in our family papers are being changed or hidden from me, like the hospital or someone in the family is sending me little messages through the records.\n- key: S021\n  name: Disorganized_Thinking_Speech\n  description: Symptoms where speech or thought appears scattered and difficult to follow due to a lack of logical or consistent flow.\n  manifestation: When I try to explain the appointments or the genealogy notes, I start with my sister’s medicine list and then I’m suddenly talking about our grandmother’s birth certificate and the county clerk, and people tell me they can’t follow what I mean.\n- key: S022\n  name: Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: I catch myself lining up papers and pill bottles over and over on the kitchen table, tapping each stack a certain number of times, even when I know I’m supposed to be getting ready to leave.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most mornings I wake up with a heavy, low feeling, like the color has gone out of the day before I even get dressed.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to look forward to watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club, but now I often leave the bag by the door and just can’t make myself care enough to go.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: My body feels slowed down lately, like it takes me twice as long to cross the room or button my sweater, though sometimes I pace around the house with the same papers in my hand.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I sit there staring at simple things, like whether to call the pharmacy or open the mail first, and my mind gets so foggy that I end up doing neither.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself, but I do find myself thinking that if I didn’t wake up one morning, it might be a relief to everyone.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: When I am in that low, colorless state, I am also most caught up in the idea that the family papers and hospital notes are carrying hidden messages for me.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the sadness and loss of interest have taken up most of this whole period since my sister became ill, even when the record worries were quieter.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n  manifestation: There was a stretch of several weeks when my mood had eased enough that I was eating and getting dressed more normally, but I still felt sure the dates and names in the records were being changed to signal something.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I have stopped teaching my craft classes and have missed calls, appointments, and family tasks I used to handle carefully. I still manage to stay in my home, but I rely more on reminders and put off things that used to be routine.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: This whole change has been going on for about a year and five months, since my sister’s health took that serious turn.\n- key: psychotic_symptoms_duration\n  name: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\n  description: 1 year and 1 month\n  manifestation: The ideas about hidden messages in the family and medical papers started about a year and a month ago.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 1 year and 2 months\n  manifestation: The low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S026\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: During this past year, has there been a period of at least two weeks where your mood or energy became noticeably worse than your usual baseline—for example, with major sleep changes, loss of energy, trouble concentrating, or thoughts of worthlessness or death?\n\nPatient: Yes. There have been stretches where it feels worse—most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like all the color has gone out of the day before I even get dressed.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating crackers and sweets like that, do you feel like you can't control or stop the eating once you start?\n\nPatient: Some days I do just keep standing there eating crackers and sweets, even though nothing really tastes right. My eating just feels all turned around.\n\nDoctor: During those worse stretches, have you had feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt about yourself?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it guilt or worthlessness exactly. I mostly just feel low and sort of empty.\n\nDoctor: During those worse stretches, have you noticed significant changes in your sleep—like sleeping much more or much less than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed a big change in my sleep, no.\n\nDoctor: When you have those worse stretches of low mood, how long do they typically last—days, or do they go on for two weeks or more at a time?\n\nPatient: It’s been going on a long time, not just days. I’d say about a year and two months now.\n\nDoctor: During those worse stretches, have you noticed a significant drop in your energy level or new difficulty concentrating on tasks?\n\nPatient: Yes, concentrating has been harder. I’ll sit there looking at something simple, like whether to call the pharmacy or open the mail first, and my mind gets so foggy I end up doing neither.\n\nDoctor: After those times when you eat the crackers and sweets like that, do you feel upset, ashamed, or distressed about having eaten that way?\n\nPatient: I do feel upset about it. It’s like my eating is all turned around—some days I forget lunch, and then other days I’m standing there eating crackers and sweets, even though nothing really tastes right.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating the crackers and sweets, do you end up eating a much larger amount than most people would in that same short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s much more than other people, exactly. I just find myself standing there eating crackers and sweets, and it doesn’t even taste right. Other days I forget lunch completely, so my eating has gotten very mixed up.\n\nDoctor: During these worse stretches, have you had any thoughts about death or that you'd be better off not being alive?\n\nPatient: I don’t have a plan to hurt myself. But sometimes I think that if I didn’t wake up one morning, it might be a relief to everyone.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
